# Windows impact of the port

Every change the macOS/Linux port made that a Windows build compiles, links or runs, with the risk if it
is wrong and how far it has been verified on Windows. It covers code inside `#ifdef _WIN32`, MSVC flags in
`CMakeLists.txt`, shared headers, and call sites whose Windows branch changed. Reviewers of the port can use
it as a checklist; [PORTING.md](../../PORTING.md) has the overview.

For most of the port no Windows machine was available, so each change was recorded here as it was made,
with what it would take to check it. A Windows x64 VM (MSVC 19.44) and, later, a Windows ARM64 VM then
worked through the list. The **How to verify** cell of each row starts with what a Windows run has shown.

## How to add a row

Add a row when you change anything a Windows build compiles, links or runs. If you are unsure whether
your change qualifies, it qualifies.

Be specific about **Risk**. "Might break" helps nobody. "If MSVC's `char16_t` and `wchar_t`
overload resolution differs here, this call binds to the wrong overload and the string is empty"
is a thing somebody can check in ninety seconds.

## Severity

- **high** — would break the Windows build, or change what the simulation computes
- **medium** — would break a Windows-only feature at runtime
- **low** — cosmetic, a warning, or a flag that moved without changing the command line

## Windows verification (2026-09-27)

Rows are counted from 1 in table order, as the cells cite them. The counts below are as of 2026-09-27;
rows added after that carry their own status.

| Mark | Rows | Meaning |
|:--|--:|:--|
| **VERIFIED** | 162 | The row's own check was done on Windows, or a stronger one. The cell names the evidence. |
| **PARTLY VERIFIED** | 63 | Some of the row's checks were done; the cell says which are left. |
| **OPEN** | 101 | None of the row's own checks has been done on Windows. |
| **nothing to verify** | 5 | The row says no Windows check applies (comments only, or a POSIX-only file). |
| **argued, not run** | 1 | No run can show it; the row's argument is the evidence. |

**The evidence the cells name:**
- **CI**: a Windows CI run (`windows-ci.ps1`) on 2026-09-27, before upstream 843bf76b's gameplay data was merged,
  so its E1 CRCs are the pins of that time (the current ones are in PORTING.md). On Windows (MSVC 19.44, x64, Release) it ran:
  - `build.bat Release`;
  - ctest, 47 of 47 in session 0 (no audio or video tests), plus `dx9_smoke`, `dx9_smoke_msaa` and
    `test_dx11device` in the desktop session;
  - E1 with seed 0 at 1200 frames (0x0177BEF6), seed 0 at 12000 (0x5273770F) and seed 1 at 12000 (0x830467DB).
    Each run played back to the same CRC, the data folder was unchanged, and Linux x86_64 gave the same
    three CRCs at the same commit.
  - "E1" in a cell means those runs.
- **VM check**: read-only checks of that CI run's Windows tree.
  The build fingerprint is 0xD883D7C9 over 3736 files on Windows, macOS and Linux alike. The checks:
  - ctest's registration (`--show-only=json-v1`);
  - the Release `.vcxproj` settings;
  - `dumpbin /dependents generals.exe`;
  - the objects built;
  - the VM's time zone;
  - the tree's `git status`;
  - an E1 run's own logs;
  - MSVC's `<thread>`;
  - `test_gameengine -V`.
- **probe**: one file, compiled by cl 19.44 on the VM, by clang on macOS and by GCC on Linux. It prints
  `Random3Class`'s tables and first draws, and compile-time facts (`_MAX_PATH`, `TCHAR`, `bool.h`).
- **dx9_smoke on the VM**: its output, read in the desktop session. The VM's CPU is GenuineIntel.
- **W2**: measurements made during the first MSVC build of the port (work item W2), each at the commit it names.
- **sized-delete run**: a Windows CI run of the change that added the sized and nothrow operators, which recompiled GameMemory.cpp.

**What is left, by what it needs.** This is the Windows risk that remains. Every PARTLY VERIFIED and OPEN row
is in exactly one line:

| Needs | Rows | Which |
|:--|--:|:--|
| A Debug build: one exists since 2026-09-29 (the standing item below), but these rows' own checks have not been run in it | 8 | 124, 145, 146, 158, 160, 204, 274, 280 |
| Another build configuration or a configure-time experiment | 8 | 13, 39, 46, 64, 67, 103, 126, 179 |
| Disassembly, timing or log comparisons against an older build | 12 | 22, 27, 40, 41, 48, 49, 65, 71, 74, 81, 90, 203 |
| A small value check nobody has written yet, or a grep | 6 | 5, 25, 28, 52, 54, 251 |
| The Direct3D 11 renderer, or both renderers, by eye | 7 | 236, 237, 238, 239, 240, 241, 247 |
| Someone at a Windows screen: menus, text, input, visuals, crash boxes (every run here is headless) | 56 | 55, 57, 58, 59, 73, 118, 134, 135, 136, 140, 143, 144, 148, 149, 150, 151, 153, 154, 155, 156, 157, 168, 171, 175, 176, 177, 178, 182, 183, 186, 187, 189, 191, 192, 193, 212, 216, 228, 229, 230, 231, 233, 242, 246, 249, 254, 256, 257, 259, 260, 261, 262, 263, 264, 269, 270 |
| A network game: two Windows machines, or Windows and a Mac | 9 | 131, 137, 142, 198, 206, 207, 219, 266, 268 |
| GameSpy, whose service is gone | 9 | 132, 138, 152, 159, 190, 194, 195, 196, 271 |
| Sound or video, which the workers never play | 4 | 108, 109, 227, 232 |
| Old data: replays or saves from before a change, a retail install, an older working tree, WorldBuilder | 12 | 107, 113, 121, 166, 197, 205, 209, 224, 234, 244, 250, 275 |
| An AMD Windows machine | 2 | 75, 78 |

Three things the checks found:
- **A Debug build did not compile on Windows.** Since 2026-09-29 an x64 Debug build does, and passes ctest and E1 (the standing item, updated below).
- **Row 15's expected figure was wrong.** The row expected -1163104121; the value is -1163302777, on every
  compiler. The code was right.
- **The Preloading memory log lines print garbage on a machine with more than 2 GB free** (row 147). They pass
  `size_t` through `%d`, as the `MEMORYSTATUS` fields did before the change. Latent and log only.

| 2026-09-22 | A2 follow-up | `Libraries/Source/Compression/ZLib/zutil.h` (vendored, not tracked) | `vendor.sh` narrows zlib's `#if defined(MACOS) \|\| defined(TARGET_OS_MAC)` at zutil.h:113 to also require `!defined(__APPLE__)`, so Darwin falls through to zutil.h's own `#define OS_CODE 0x03 /* assume Unix */` and keeps the real `fdopen`. Measured before and after: `OS_CODE` 0x07 -> 0x03, `fdopen` macro -> not a macro. | Neither `MACOS` nor `TARGET_OS_MAC` is ever defined on Windows, so that branch was already dead there and the added condition is never evaluated. Windows keeps `OS_CODE 0x0b` from the `WIN32` branch above it. The risk is that this reasoning is wrong about some MSVC configuration that does define one of them - in which case Windows would move from 0x07 to 0x0b and gain a working `fdopen`, which is a fix, not a regression. | low | **Windows: VERIFIED** (VM check: the `compression` target defines neither `MACOS` nor `TARGET_OS_MAC`; CI: it builds.) `grep -rn MACOS` the Windows build's command lines; confirm neither name is defined. Then build `compression`. |
| 2026-09-22 | persistfactory | `WWSaveLoad/persistfactory.h` | `SimplePersistFactoryClass::Save` writes the object-identity token as `PersistClass *` at `sizeof(objptr)` instead of `(uint32)obj` at `sizeof(uint32)`. Load is unchanged - it already read `sizeof(T *)`. On Win64 the chunk payload goes from 4 bytes to 8. | This CHANGES what a Windows build writes into that chunk, so it is a format change on the one platform nobody here can run. The reason it is safe is that the format it replaces cannot be read by the build that writes it: `ChunkLoadClass::Read` refuses a read that overruns the chunk and returns 0 without touching the buffer, so Win64 Load has always left `old_obj` at NULL and registered every object under the key NULL. Measured with the real ChunkSaveClass/ChunkLoadClass and the real template: payload 4, Read returns 0, `Register_Pointer` receives NULL; after the fix, payload 8, Read returns 8, correct address. There is no working Win64 save of this shape to be compatible with. A Win32 build stays symmetric at 4 bytes and is unaffected. | medium | **Windows: VERIFIED** (CI: test_wwsaveload, which holds `persist_factory_writes_a_pointer_token_load_can_read`.) Build `test_wwsaveload` on Windows and run `persist_factory_writes_a_pointer_token_load_can_read`. It goes red on the old code on x64 and green on the new. |
| 2026-09-22 | test audit | `GeneralsMD/Code/CMakeLists.txt` | `add_lib_test()` now always calls `add_test()`; a `PENDING_MACOS` target is registered `DISABLED` instead of being skipped. Line counts are unchanged and no other backslash in the tree was touched. | A no-op on Windows by construction: MSVC resolves `"Common\Foo.h"` and `"Common/Foo.h"` to the same file, so the preprocessor sees identical content and emits an identical object. The change would only bite if some path resolved to a *different* file under the two spellings, which needs two headers differing only by separator - impossible, a separator cannot be in a filename. | low | **Windows: VERIFIED** (CI: build.) | 2026-09-22 | B10 | `WWLib/bittype.h` | `uint32` changed from `unsigned long` to `uint32_t`, `sint32` from `signed long` to `int32_t`. **Width on Windows is unchanged** (LLP64 `long` is already 32 bits); the *type identity* is not. | `unsigned long` and `unsigned int` are distinct types even where both are 32 bits, so this can change overload resolution, template specialisation and `printf`-format diagnostics on MSVC. A function overloaded on `unsigned long` and `unsigned int` binds differently; `%lu` over a `uint32` becomes a format mismatch; a `template<> struct X<unsigned long>` specialisation stops matching. None of this is a size problem and none of it shows up by checking sizes. Measured on macOS: across the 34 objects that currently compile, the warning set is **byte-identical** before and after (104 either way), and the only error that changes is `test_w3dlayout` going green. But that is 34 objects out of 701 uses in 98 files — most of WWVegas cannot compile here yet (`windows.h`, B5), so the sweep is real but thin, and MSVC diagnoses some of this differently in any case. | **high** | **Windows: PARTLY VERIFIED** (CI: build, test_w3dlayout. Left: the grep for specialisations and `%l` formats over `uint32`/`sint32`.) Do NOT check `sizeof`. Full Windows build with warnings up, then grep for template specialisations over `uint32`/`sint32`, and for `printf`/`sprintf`/`DEBUG_LOG` format strings using `%l` with a `uint32` argument. `ctest -R test_w3dlayout` must be green. |

(2) `snprintf` against `lstrcpyn`: the same bytes for n > 0 on a C99 `snprintf` (MSVC 2015 and later); an older CRT's `_snprintf`-like `snprintf` would not terminate on truncation. (3) `strlen` where `lstrlen` took NULL: `lstrlen(NULL)` is 0, `strlen(NULL)` faults; the three sites are guarded or never NULL, by reading. (4) `missingtexture.cpp`'s arrays now external: a clash with another object's `missing_image_pixels` would fail the link, loudly; there is none in the tree. (5) The `: int` on `GraphicsVenderID` and `waveType`: MSVC's unscoped enums are `int` already, so no layout moves. (6) The include-order change in the draw modules: MSVC compiled the old order, and the new one is the order every other draw module uses. (7) The two pointer-truncation fixes: `surfaceclass.cpp`'s changes behaviour only above 4 GB, from a fault to the right answer, and only `Font3D` (never created by the game) reaches it; `assetmgr.cpp`'s gives the same number it always did. ## Standing items

These do not belong to one change.

| Item | Why it matters |
|:--|:--|
| **Suspected live defect: texture stage 2 is invisible to the D3D11 backend.** | **Windows, 2026-09-29: closed, unreachable.** `shader.cpp:955-963` and `:1002-1014` do set stage 2 through raw `DX8CALL`, past the wrapper and the Direct3D 11 mirrors. But both run only when `voodoo3` is true, and `shader.cpp:553-554` sets it only for a 3dfx Voodoo 3: the vendor 3dfx (`dx8caps.cpp:85`, PCI vendor 0x121A, or `:571-573`, an unknown vendor whose driver DLL's name starts with '3') and the device 0x0005 (`dx8caps.cpp:410`). A Voodoo 3 cannot create a Direct3D 11 device. The only other way in is an adapter whose vendor that table does not know, with a driver DLL named '3...' and the device ID 0x0005; short of that, under `-dx11` stage 2 is never set and the backend misses nothing. The real gap found on the way is the texture-stage transforms (`D3DTS_TEXTUREn`), in the ledger. |
| **E1 cannot do its job — but E3 now covers one half of it.** | **Windows, 2026-09-27: done for Intel.** W2 and the 2026-09-27 CI run, on the data before upstream 843bf76b: Windows x64 (MSVC) gives the CRCs macOS arm64 and Linux x86_64 give, and replays cross between them with no mismatch. On AMD the D3DX body question is open (rows 75, 78). The determinism check was designed as a cross-platform checksum comparison: same seed, Windows and macOS, same result. With one platform it can only prove the Mac build is self-consistent and record a checksum for a future comparison. That is worth doing and is **not** the same thing. The question E1 exists to answer — does this port desync against Windows — stays open until a Windows machine exists. Nothing downstream of M1 should be described as validated until it is.<br><br>**What E3 covers, as of 2026-09-22:** the *architecture* half. `ctest -R arch_differential` builds one source for arm64 and x86_64 and runs both, so x86-versus-arm differences — float-to-int rounding, FMA contraction, the width of `long` — are caught here and now. It is green on 170 rows including every DetTrig output and an 80,000-value sweep fingerprint, and it names 12 rows that differ. **What E3 does not cover:** the *compiler* half. Both binaries are clang's. MSVC's codegen, its optimiser, its `/fp:` semantics and its 32-bit `long` remain entirely unverified, and the x86_64 column of E3's own table is not a Windows answer. This item stays open. |
| **`replay-check.ps1` cannot run.** | **Windows, 2026-09-27: done.** It runs in every Windows CI run, through `windows-ci.ps1`. PowerShell, and it is the tool `CONTRIBUTING.md` names for proving a change leaves replays and network games alone. Any change reaching `GameLogic` or the maths under it is unverified against it. |
| **The MSVC flag semantics in `CMakeLists.txt` are unverified.** | **Windows, 2026-09-27: checked.** gameengine's Release project carries /W3, /permissive, /EHa, /Oy-, /Zi, /FS, /MP and the static CRT, and generals.exe imports no VC runtime DLL (rows 7, 8). A1 moves `/EHa`, `/Oy-`, `/Zi`, `/FS`, `/MP` and `CMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY` behind a platform guard. The intent is that Windows produces an identical compiler command line. Nobody has seen one. A1 left the `if(MSVC)` block itself byte for byte as it was and added an `elseif` after it, so the flags are not the risk; the two settings that moved above `project()` are, and they have a row of their own. |
| **The first Windows build after A1 will fail on `make -k`-shaped grounds, not on flags.** | **Windows, 2026-09-27: it happened and is fixed.** W2: 63 errors, then 19 more once ATL was in (W2). A1's macOS branch is additive, but A1 also proved that `wwdebug`, `wwutil` and `compression` do not compile under clang at all. Nothing about that touches Windows — and it does mean that the next person to diff the two platforms is comparing a working build against a build that stops in its first library. Do not read that as a regression. |
| **A `Debug` or `_INTERNAL` build of this tree does not compile on x64, and did not before this port started.** | **Windows, 2026-09-29: done for x64 Debug; `_INTERNAL` not built.** The build and what it then found are fixed: a Debug CI run on the Windows CI VM (`windows-ci.ps1 -Config Debug`): the build, ctest 50 of 50 with order_cameos_selfcheck skipped (no numpy there), the GPU tests, and E1 0@1200 playing back to 0xE5C34BF3, Release's pin. Before that, as found on 2026-09-27: `windows-ci.ps1 -Config Debug` on 2026-09-27 stops in `wwdebug` at `Debug.h:244`. A port change spelled `SimpleProfiler`'s four counters `Int64`, which wwdebug's files do not declare; `__int64` was the compiler's own. Only wwdebug compiles `DEBUG_PROFILE`, because only it lacks `RELEASE_DEBUG_LOGGING`. Three Debug-only test links fail too: `GameEngineDebug.lib` (gametext_csf, test_premain_strings, test_premain_unicode), `debugdebug.lib` (test_debug), and `DebugLog` (test_wwdownload). The fix is in this tree now. `GameCommon.h:59` defines `DUMP_PERF_STATS` in every `_DEBUG` or `_INTERNAL` build. `PerfTimer.h` compiled `GetPrecisionTimer` under that define, and its body was an `__asm RDTSC` block — `USE_QPF` was never defined, so the assembly was the live branch, not the dead one. This project is x64 only (`CMakeLists.txt:48-52` refuses anything else) and **MSVC does not accept `__asm` on x64 at all**. `-DPERF_TIMERS=ON` reaches the same code in a Release build. Found by B2, which has replaced the block with the clock shim, so the configurations should build again — but nobody has confirmed either the breakage or the fix on a Windows machine, and the conclusion rests on reading the preprocessor rather than on a compiler's answer. **It also means an early claim that "every remaining `__asm` block is inside `#if 0`" was wrong**; it has been corrected. The only other `__asm` in the tree, `WWMath/vp.cpp`, is expanded solely inside `#if defined(__ICL)` and is genuinely dead. |
| **`#pragma pack` layout is assumed, not measured, on MSVC.** | **Windows, 2026-09-27: checked.** The B4 and L1 `sizeof`/`offsetof` asserts compile under MSVC (rows 208, 248, 267). B4 adds `sizeof`/`offsetof` asserts. They will be checked by a clang build only. If MSVC disagrees on any packed struct, the asserts fire there and the build fails loudly — which is the good outcome, but it happens on someone else's machine, later. |
